> For current upstream v2 changes and marker links, use the [current shared-change inventory](v2-current-shared-change-inventory.md). This document preserves its original assessment scope and counts.

# V1 Kilo marker port assessment

> Completed source-assessment inventory: all 816 rows have a disposition. This is a read-only port assessment, not implementation or runtime acceptance. Partial and needs-port rows retain the specific gaps described below; no capability completion credit is added.

## Totals

Pinned v1 `d99662338e3ddbd2613ab41fc0369837a6eb4be9`; 816 files / 6128 occurrences. Dispositions: already ported 65; deferred 68; native equivalent 137; needs port 163; not needed 150; partial 233; unresolved 0.

## Final TUI assessment

The final 23 TUI rows were assessed directly from pinned marker blocks against current v2 runtime, plugin, and test consumers. Mixed legacy surfaces remain partial where concrete host/native behavior coexists with missing Kilo invariants; session scope switching, list-level empty-state actions, export options, and Kilo presentation regressions are explicit needs-port items rather than inferred from adjacent UI.

## Completed packages/opencode and docs-automation handoffs

An initial packages/opencode handoff was withdrawn after semantic review established that it used path/file-presence heuristics and generic rationales; all affected rows were restored before the replacement worker re-audited the complete slice from marker invariants and concrete consumers. The docs-automation handoff assessed 15 rows as deferred because pinned v1 workflows directly consume automation modules absent from v2; the packages/kilo-docs tree is also absent, so adjacent runtime features are not treated as parity. Nine historical authored-plan rows are not needed because their marker-only statements are non-executable completed-work notes and v2 has active ownership rules.

## TUI data projection and sync fixtures

V2 replaces the monolithic SyncProvider with location-keyed client projections. Most session, shell/background, VCS, config, indexing, and owned Kilo consumers are native or relocated, while network-wait/tool-terminal state, released-v1 Sync folding, and generic subscriber isolation remain gaps, making the source partial. The old fixture nesting is obsolete; the workspace VCS regression is native through location-keyed storage and the current data test.

## TUI event routing pair

Native v2 event shapes remove the legacy sync-envelope adapter and preserve location metadata, but direct `useEvent` subscribers are no longer filtered to the current project. The paired v2 suite adds strong reconnect and durable-event coverage while omitting the old foreign-project rejection case, so source is partial and the isolation regression needs a port.

## Mixed TUI status and footer surfaces

All four rows are partial. V2 removes obsolete plugin-path parsing and preserves typed MCP status, child-session state, navigation, location, and Kilo sidebar branding through native or owned seams. Missing invariants remain explicit: Kilo version/config paths and auth commands in the status dialog, free-model onboarding, remote-presence indication, and the agent-colored subagent footer spinner.

## TUI connectivity and branded guidance tests

Typed integration connections natively supersede v1's provider-ID/cost heuristic, with renamed tests covering credential and environment connections. Kilo-specific model/MCP error guidance and its tests remain unported. The old provider ordering regression also lacks a v2 policy or consumer-backed equivalent.

## TUI session utilities and small dialog slice

Rename completion and Kilo sidebar branding are native equivalents through durable data and owned slots. Network recovery, multiline alerts, and fixed-width Markdown tables need ports. Dialog centering and embedded terminals are partial: v2 exposes stronger generic primitives, but does not preserve the old global/default or tool-snapshot-specific behavior.

## TUI config, bindings, themes, and path formatting

V2 natively strengthens portable home-path formatting, while the old cross-tree TypeScript aliases and a Kilo-branded internal mode-stack error are not required under the owned-package architecture. Kilo title/Vim/attention config is partial. Feedback, Vim-toggle and news bindings, the Kilo/colorblind theme catalogue, and the Kilo default-sound regression remain unported; generic input, feedback colors, branded logo tokens, or static footer content are not treated as parity.

## Focused TUI notifications, branding, and tips

Kilo's logo and exit epilogue were relocated cleanly into the owned `packages/kilo-cli` TUI plugin, with renderer and resume-command tests; their removed shared markers do not represent lost behavior. The old home-tip catalogue and persistent toggle have no v2 consumer and need an owned plugin contribution. Notifications are only partial: v2 has stronger lifecycle infrastructure, but currently treats interruption as completion, sounds for completed subagents, and lacks Kilo's manual-abort and timeout-message policy; the corresponding old regression cases need a port.

## Shared TUI attention and dialog slice

The v2 agent dialog preserves display labels with canonical IDs under its renamed schema. Attention behavior is stronger and tested, but Kilo title/sound-pack branding is absent. The Kilo-pricing-specific retry/upgrade dialog branch has no current component and needs a port.

## Focused TUI service slice

The cost-nudge, thumbs-feedback and no-display link-dialog consumers have no v2 equivalents and need owned TUI ports. The ordered embedded-worker helper is architecture-obsolete rather than deferred: v2 service/interactive-server ownership preserves ordered shutdown without that worker. Native projection tests cover much of old SyncV2, while its resolved-project filtering proof remains unlocated.

## Delegation and web-search tool slice

Native v2 subagents cover durable child execution and configurable depth, but only partially replace Kilo's task policy because persisted model precedence and inherited denial ceilings are not proven. The interactive Agent Manager's batching/worktrees/sections/events need a port. V2's provider abstraction can host a Kilo search integration, but Tavily does not replace the marked authenticated Kilo Exa route and response/error contract.

## Missing specialized-tool slice

Chart rendering, image generation/editing, and outbound remote file delivery have no current v2 tool consumers. The paired image and file-delivery regressions also need ports: generic MCP output and inbound user attachments do not cover their renderer, provider, filesystem-authorization, or delivery-only invariants.

## Remote ownership source slice

V2 natively replaces the extracted attached-session state machine with heartbeat reconciliation, detach fencing and reconnect deduplication in the owned relay. The broader sender and WebSocket files are partial because their active command/heartbeat behavior is present while spawn-capable instance creation and advertisement are absent; the standalone instance-advertisement file is deferred with that retired product surface.

## Remote command/protocol and delivery-contract slice

The v2 relay natively preserves the unconditional exit capability and detach semantics. Heartbeats, reconnect and presence are present, while the old spawn-capable instance advertisement is intentionally absent. Prompt enqueue dismisses remote suggestions, but exact question dismissal is unproven; likewise, typed user attachment handling does not prove the old `send_file` delivery-only replay exclusion.

## Plan and historical-recall tool slice

Native v2 `plan_exit` policy and tests supersede the v1 forwarding hook. Kilo memory provides a permissioned recall subset, but it is not equivalent to v1's arbitrary historical session transcript search/read across the worktree family; the missing transcript-specific behavior and regression tests are recorded separately.

## Network and overflow session slice

The v1 offline wait/reconnect state machine and race-focused tests need a Kilo-owned v2 port; generic retry and simulation networking are not equivalent. V2 has provider-driven context-overflow compaction and extensive runner tests, but the pinned Kilo token counter and proactive/economic threshold are not proven, so overflow is partial.

## Session execution follow-on and claim refinements

Legacy run-state/status/todo Layer composition is superseded by native v2 execution and projection services; workflow-specific variant precedence remains unported. Sharing claims are narrowed to local-fixture acceptance because deployed viewer/team/incremental-sync gates were not exercised. Variant persistence is native: Kilo injects its `kilo2/<profile>` state root at runtime, overriding the generic source comment.

## Permission and variant follow-on

The v2 mini TUI carries the permission state machine and model-variant selection logic. Permission remains partial because skill-shell requests no longer remove persistent approval from the shared footer. Variant persistence is native because Kilo runtime storage injection overrides the generic upstream path comment.

## Suggestions, sharing and sandbox batch

Suggestion lifecycle, tool admission, replay and accept/dismiss behavior are carried by the owned v2 remote adapter and its integration tests. Kilo public sharing is Gateway-backed and tested end to end. Sandbox is intentionally partial: v2 has launch-scoped shell/local-MCP OS confinement and PTY refusal, not v1's persisted per-session toggle, inheritance and all-file-tool decoration; the reusable Linux CI setup action is also absent.

## Memory/usage and initial remote-control batch

V2 replaces parent-message cost propagation with durable root-and-descendant aggregation, carries throughput in the native TUI session-row seam, and replaces memory HTTP routes with location-scoped Memory RPC backed by the owned memory engine. The relay adapter preserves protocol-v1 command and detach invariants, but the old standalone `kilo remote` spawn-capable host and instance advertisement are intentionally outside the current preview surface, so those rows remain partial.

## Account/Gateway/model-policy batch

Direct source review mapped the v1 Core Kilo provider hook to the location/account-scoped Gateway plugin, model catalog, request isolation and routed-model seams. The large v1 owned provider helper remains partial because its Gateway behaviors are ported but unrelated custom loaders are mixed into the same file. Credential lifecycle is partial pending presence-side reconciliation. The old backend model-search helper has shipped client equivalents in native CLI search and the original VS Code model-selector utilities. Four coherent v1 tests map to current Gateway/Core tests for auth fallback, local providers, cache isolation and failed-catalog withdrawal.

## Previously completed batches

Packages/UI: 54 ported. Marker-bearing packages/kilo-*: indexing query ported, policy native equivalent, VS Code manifest partial guard, UI contract partial test coverage. Config/settings/import: 15 source-backed dispositions.
